Strong directions are sample/state-specific descriptors, method-tagged measurements, explicit source evidence and missingness, ordered heterostructure components, and a strict distinction between measured sample structures and database references. Recommended refinements: use one versioned descriptor schema but separate requested-target fields from observed-product fields to avoid target leakage; preserve all method-specific values instead of forcing a universal headline size or composition; retain raw source wording alongside any normalized value; and distinguish not reported, not reviewed, and unresolved sample linkage. Pilot and audit the descriptor before bulk adoption.

## 2026-09-22 — Structure recipe counter clarified and eligibility issue identified

User asked why Synthesis records shows zero verified sample-coordinate–recipe pairs and how to improve it. Read the actual `scripts/dataset_lib.py` eligibility rule and canonical structure assets. The counter is the number admitted to `exact_structure_recipe`, requiring source review, nonduplicate status, curator-qualified measured coordinates, explicit product-to-recipe linkage, task selection, and recipe completeness. It does not require coordinates for every atom in a finite nanocrystal; an appropriately qualified sample-specific periodic refinement can be relevant. A downloaded bulk reference or illustrative particle cannot establish such a pair.

The current gate is overly broad: it rejects any `quality.missing_fields` and any `not_reported` status anywhere in materials/stocks/operations, including optional or characterization details. The metric therefore conflates verified linkage with task-specific completeness, and its generic label is misleading. This is an identified implementation issue, not a claim that changing the filter alone will produce valid pairs. Evans species-9 records contain a source-derived molecular crystallographic model, explicitly excluded from measured-label training; the crystallization record has explicit linkage but incomplete conditions and no requested training task. It is not QD atomic structure evidence.

Proposed redesign addition: distinguish sample-specific coordinates available, source-verified structure–recipe linkage, and eligibility for a defined training task; report individual exclusion reasons; type periodic refinements, finite particles, molecular structures and external references separately; assess required fields by task rather than all missing metadata. Prioritize existing main/SI/deposition evidence with verifiable sample labels, use independently audited mapping, and retain composition/phase/size/morphology/property-conditioned recipe tasks while coordinate-paired coverage grows. Website/data unchanged in this explanatory turn; no new paper admission or download.

## 2026-09-20 — Real world impact discussion without proposal revision

User explicitly requested discussion of the academic and industrial potential of designing nanocrystals to specification, with no revision to the RCC proposal. Assessment: the strongest value is reliable synthesis of feasible target combinations spanning composition, phase, particle dimensions/distributions, interfaces, defects, surface chemistry and application performance. A unit cell alone is insufficient. Academic opportunities include controlled structure–property tests, access to otherwise difficult materials, reproducibility and experimentally validated discovery. Commercial QD display materials and colloidal-QD infrared cameras establish existing applications; research reagents are also commercial. Catalyst examples illustrate research opportunities, not demonstrated industrial deployment. Successful particle synthesis, device integration and economical manufacturing remain separate accomplishments.

For MatterSyn, the recommended impact test is prospective success at meeting a predeclared materials specification, fewer experiments/time per successful target, independent batch/lab reproducibility, and measured cost/yield/stability where available. These are proposed evaluation criteria, not achieved results. No market valuation or guaranteed universal synthesis capability is claimed. Proposal and paper-review pause remain unchanged.

Primary sources consulted: robotic colloidal nanocrystal synthesis https://www.nature.com/articles/s44160-023-00250-5 ; Nanosys 2025 display/manufacturing report https://www.nanosys.com/blog-newsroom/quantum-dots-take-center-stage-at-display-week-2025 ; SWIR Vision commercial CQD cameras https://www.swirvisionsystems.com/about/ ; Thermo Fisher Qdot research reagent https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/Q10103MP ; colloidal Cu catalyst stability study https://www.nature.com/articles/s41563-024-01819-x . Manufacturer statements establish product availability, not independent validation of every advertised performance claim or of MatterSyn itself.

## Model-training objective and dataset design — September 16, 2026

- User states that the collected information will train a **small model to propose synthesis recipes for a specified crystal structure**, with **5,000–50,000 data points** and an intended **one website/page per data point**. - Earlier design recommendation (implemented in pilot above): keep material/method hubs as indexes; generate one record page per source-linked experiment or recipe variant from canonical structured data. Link batch, aliquot/fraction, sample and measurement IDs. One paper may supply multiple records, and one method-family page need not be a complete experiment.
- Before the pilot migration, the site was a useful review interface, but its data contained display strings for quantities and study-level evidence without complete recipe-to-sample mappings. Normalize numeric values/units/ranges and operations with material-flow dependencies; retain source text, missingness, inherited fields and source provenance. Treat unknowns as unknown, not zero or model-filled experimental truth.
- Essential current limitation: Murray figures are not all mapped to exact recipes, Method 2 lacks quantities, and the external COD unit cell / illustrative 582-atom crop are not measured atomic structures of a specified Murray batch. They must not be converted automatically into verified exact-CIF-to-complete-recipe supervision.
- Proposed model conditioning should include requested composition/phase/structure and, where relevant, size/morphology/surface state and laboratory constraints; structure alone does not select a unique colloidal recipe. Keep desired targets separate from observed products and partial/failure outcomes. Chemical intuition remains separately labeled evidence/interpretation; no automatic use of generated explanations as gold training targets.
- Recommend task-specific eligibility, paper/recipe-family/batch grouped splits and deduplication, a diverse curated pilot before scaling, retrieval/simple-model baselines, adaptation of a pretrained small model where appropriate, and prospective lab validation. Dataset size alone does not establish sufficient independent supervision or reliable success probabilities.

ACS Nano 2025, 3,508 colloidal recipes across 348 compositions for forward size/shape prediction (https://pubs.acs.org/doi/10.1021/acsnano.5c09134). The latter does not establish general inverse full-recipe generation.

### Murray Methods 1 and 2, upstream preparation and storage

- The full 1993 main article is verified; **matching SI remains not located/verified and has not been used**. Do not claim it never existed. Method 1 is the explicit TOPSe route. Method 2 replaces the phosphine selenide with `(TMS)2Se` and refers back to the Method 1 framework; it does not independently quantify the CdSe charge, stock composition/concentration, ordinary injection/growth temperature, duration or yield.
- Method 2's approximately 100 °C injection/growth example concerns the smallest approximately 12 Å (1.2 nm) species. The 290–320 °C range in that paragraph concerns **CdS**, not CdSe. Shared Method 1 context and common nanocrystal workup must be labeled inherited/general, not independently measured Method 2 run parameters. Paper-wide characterization is not automatically a Method 2 outcome.
- Murray p. 8706 explicitly stores prepared `(TMS)2Se` and `(BDMS)2Te` at **−35 °C in a drybox**. Duration and drybox gas composition are unreported; this does not specify final CdSe storage. The joint citation to references 3a and 4 does not establish an exclusive one-reference-per-compound mapping.
- Verified upstream primary source: Steigerwald et al., *Surface Derivatization and Isolation of Semiconductor Cluster Molecules*, JACS **1988**, 110, 3046–3050, DOI `10.1021/ja00218a008`. Murray's bibliography prints 1987, but the original confirms 1988. Main PDF is `research-assets/steigerwald1988-main.pdf`; printed p. 3047 is PDF page 3 because of a repository cover.
- That page reports a limited precursor example: Se 0.53 g (6.7 mg-atom = 6.7 mmol), lithium triethylborohydride solution in THF 13 mL (13 mmol), and chlorotrimethylsilane 1.45 g (13 mmol), yielding an approximately 0.3 M `(TMS)2Se` solution in THF. Temperature, duration, full addition order, total THF volume and isolated yield are not supplied in that precursor sentence. Do not borrow the downstream HgSe preparation's conditions or yield. This is a separate cited preparation, **not Murray's established injection-stock composition**.
- The 1988 general storage statement is 0 °C in a drybox; Murray's own value remains −35 °C. Detty and Seidler, J. Org. Chem. 1982, 47, 1354–1356, DOI `10.1021/jo00346a041`, has verified bibliographic identity but **the full original procedure was not accessed**. A later Beecher 2014 Li2Se/TMSBr route was reviewed separately; it must not replace the 1988 TMSCl chemistry or fill Murray omissions.
- Detailed source audit and missing fields: `research-assets/murray1993-precursor-method2-review.json`. The existing 51 mL nominal component sum versus reported 50 mL syringe discrepancy remains unresolved. Derived stock concentrations are labeled nominal and assume additive volumes; optional pyridine exchange remains a separate branch.

### Structural downloads and chemical intuition

- Final structures distinguishes a bulk **unit cell** from a **finite illustrative particle**, alongside original measured figures. COD **9016056** is the experimental bulk reference (wurtzite P6₃mc, a = b = 4.299 Å, c = 7.010 Å; 2 Cd + 2 Se per cell). Materials Project **mp-1070** is a separately linked computed reference, not the source of those experimental values or a measurement of these nanocrystals.
- Unit-cell boundary coordination is shown with optional periodic neighbors. The 3.5 × 3.0 nm Figure 6 envelope produces an illustrative 582-atom crop, Cd288Se294; this count is geometric, not measured sample stoichiometry. It omits reported stacking faults, ligands, surface relaxation and actual facets. The particle CIF's 80 Å orthogonal vacuum box is artificial, not the CdSe unit cell.
- Downloads in `recipe-atlas/dist/assets/cdse-structures/`: original COD CIF, explicitly expanded P1 unit-cell CIF, finite-cluster XYZ and vacuum-cell CIF, plus `download-manifest.json` with provenance and validation. Later experimental systems do not establish the original specimens' kinetics, impurity composition or surface coverage, and outlook is not a novelty claim.

Bawendi, *Synthesis and Characterization of Nearly Monodisperse CdE (E = S, Se, Te) Semiconductor Nanocrystallites*, JACS 115, 8706–8715, DOI `10.1021/ja00072a025`.
- Filename, DOI-list and download-log searches initially found neither. The verified original was obtained from MIT's hosted copy, inspected across all 10 pages, and added to `downloaded_papers/10.1021_ja00072a025.pdf`. Working source and renders are in `research-assets/murray1993-*`.

An asynchronous filename/location question was sent to the user and was unanswered when this update was written. Do not substitute a supplement from a later paper that cites this one.
- The earlier home page featured CdSe **Method 1**, followed through its 10-mL-aliquot isolation and separate size-selective precipitation. In the latest local architecture, it has its own method route linked from the CdSe hub. Alivisatos 2000 is preserved at `alivisatos-2000.html`; 2017 CdSe core page remains at `nakonechnyi-2017.html`.
- Key synthesis: 50 g TOPO dried/degassed at ≈200 °C, ≈1 Torr, ≈20 min with periodic argon; stabilize at ≈300 °C under ≈1 atm Ar. Solution A: 1.00 mL Me2Cd (13.35 mmol) +25.0 mL TOP. Solution B:10.0 mL 1.0 M TOPSe (10.00 mmol)+15.0 mL TOP. Combine in drybox and load the reported 50-mL syringe. Nominal component volumes total51 mL; preserve the ambiguity, not an invented measured injection volume.
- Single rapid injection with heat removed, vigorous stirring; ≈300→≈180 °C drop and 440–460 nm initial absorption feature. Restore heat, gradually raise to230–260 °C, monitor absorption every5–10min. Lower temperature if size distribution broadens, raise if growth stalls, withdraw by cannula at desired absorption. Duration is a few hours without a fixed size/time schedule. Method1 yields a sampled major-axis range≈1.5–11.5nm.
- Workup basis10mL: cool≈60°C;20mL methanol→flocculate, centrifuge and keep flocculate;25mL1-butanol→centrifuge, keep clear supernatant and discard gray Cd/Se byproduct;25mL methanol→reflocculate;50mL methanol rinse and vacuum dry. ≈300mg is capped product from this aliquot, not whole-batch yield or bare-CdSe mass. Centrifugation speed/time and drying time/temperature are unspecified.
- Size selection: purified particles in anhydrous1-butanol; add anhydrous methanol dropwise to persistent opalescence; centrifuge; precipitate enriched in largest particles; repeat until no further spectral sharpening. Figure1 example≈3.5nm±10%→≈3.7nm±5%, not universal cycle count.
- Crystal view uses Figure6 TEM dimensions3.5×3.0nm (long±5%,short±6%). Illustration uses a bulk wurtzite ellipsoid without the actual stacking faults/cap/facets. Paper Figure5 optical sample≈3.5nm has≈9.6%PLQY relative toRhodamine640; identity with Figure6 sample is unknown, so optical and TEM evidence are kept separately labeled.

## Research landscape discussed

The discussion surveyed literature available through September 2026. The central opportunity is connecting AI-generated candidates to reliable physical predictions, workable synthesis routes, and measured performance. Predicted stability, reproducible synthesis, and useful device performance are distinct achievements.

Six active directions were identified:

1. **General-purpose atomistic models.** Models such as UMA predict energies and forces across materials, molecules, and catalysts. Important unresolved issues include unfamiliar chemistry, interfaces, nonequilibrium configurations, and long-range charge/polarization. Broad chemical coverage does not guarantee application-specific accuracy. References: [UMA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23971); [learning electrical response](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41524-025-01911-z).
2. **Inverse design with multiple constraints.** Generative models propose compositions and structures for target properties. Chemeleon2 uses reinforcement learning to balance novelty, predicted stability, diversity, and properties. Optimization can exploit scoring-model errors, and computational stability does not establish synthesizability. Reference: [Chemeleon2, July 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42256-026-01262-4).
3. **Synthesis planning and experimental feedback.** DiffSyn generates alternative zeolite recipes and demonstrated an experimental synthesis. A separate perovskite study combined active learning and automated fabrication with measured device results. Remaining problems include kinetics, competing phases, failed-experiment data, and recipe transfer across labs. References: [DiffSyn](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-025-00949-9); [closed-loop perovskite discovery](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10482-y).
4. **Models of actual samples and microstructure.** Nominally identical materials can differ because of defects, phase purity, grain structure, and processing history. EXIT incorporates experimental X-ray diffraction into MOF property prediction; PolyMicros models three-dimensional polycrystalline microstructure. Connecting processing, microstructure, and performance remains an opportunity. References: [EXIT](https://doi.org/10.1021/acscentsci.6c01049); [PolyMicros](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11055).
5. **More accurate electronic physics.** Neural wavefunctions offer a route beyond simply reproducing approximate density-functional-theory labels. Transferable wavefunctions for solids are promising but remain limited in demonstrated scope and computational affordability. Reference: [transferable neural wavefunctions](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-025-00872-z).
6. **Trustworthy extrapolation and evaluation.** Models need to work on unfamiliar chemistry and express useful uncertainty. UniFFBench reports gaps between computational and experimental performance. A comparison of crystal generation methods found strong simple ion-substitution baselines, emphasizing the need to distinguish stability, structural novelty, and property targeting. References: [UniFFBench](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-026-01019-4); [generative-model baselines](https://doi.org/10.1039/D5MH00010F).

The assistant's strongest research bets were synthesis models that account for kinetics, uncertainty-guided experiments, and models connecting processing to microstructure and performance.

## Positioning a synthesis-recipe project

**AI for materials synthesis recipes is an active frontier, but plausible recipe generation already has substantial prior art.** A strong contribution should demonstrate an improvement in experimental success, efficiency, reproducibility, or generalization.

Two relevant precedents discussed:

- **DiffSyn**, published in Nature Computational Science in February 2026, models alternative synthesis routes for a desired zeolite and includes experimental validation. [Paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-025-00949-9).
- **Closed-Loop Solid-State Synthesis Planning for Materials Discovery With Large Language Models**, published in Advanced Materials in August 2026, combines literature retrieval, recipe generation, and experimental feedback for inorganic synthesis. Feedback or retrieval plus an LLM alone should not be treated as a new contribution. [Paper](https://doi.org/10.1002/adma.74502).

### Proposed research question

> Can an AI learn from observed impurity phases and processing history to reach a desired material in fewer experiments?

A motivating example is an experiment that produces the intended phase plus unwanted phases. The system would use the measured result to recommend the next precursor combination or processing change, with uncertainty estimates.

The exact novelty needs a targeted literature check after selecting the material family. This is a proposed hypothesis, not a claim of an unstudied method or guaranteed publication.

## Recommended initial scope

- Choose **one material family and one synthesis method** based on the collaborator's equipment and expertise.
- A family of ternary oxides made by solid-state synthesis was offered as a working example, not a selected system.
- Define the target by composition, crystal phase, and an observable outcome such as phase purity.
- Initially vary precursor choice and only two or three controllable processing variables. Control and record the remaining context.
- If the collaborator specializes in solution synthesis, retain the overall planning framework and adapt the recipe variables to that method.
- Select the scientific objective and feasible experimental budget before choosing a complex model architecture.

## Data strategy

Represent a recipe as **structured, ordered operations linked to the result of that particular experiment**. Useful fields include:

- Target composition and crystal phase.
- Precursors, amounts/ratios, purity, particle size where available, and batch size.
- Mixing, thermal profile, holding times, cooling, atmosphere, and relevant vessel/equipment context.
- Observed products, impurity phases, quantitative phase fractions or other target properties, and measurement uncertainty.
- Source paper or experiment identifier, provenance, and explicitly marked missing information.

### Public starting resource

The December 2025 Scientific Data paper **Text-mined dataset of solid-state syntheses with impurity phases using Large Language Model** provides **80,806 syntheses**, including **18,869 with reported impurity phases**.

- [Dataset paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41597-025-06222-y)
- [Downloadable JSON](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.30423274)

Important interpretation: **no impurity reported does not mean verified phase purity**. Literature records can omit failures or relevant conditions and contain extraction errors. Audit the chemistry-specific subset and supplement it with the collaborator's complete records, including unsuccessful, partial, and ambiguous outcomes. Unobserved recipes are not known failures.

## Suggested modeling approach

The core prediction is:

**Target + recipe + laboratory context → distribution of possible experimental outcomes**

Three components were proposed:

1. **Recipe proposer:** retrieve related syntheses and propose several feasible precursor/process combinations.
2. **Outcome predictor:** estimate product phases, phase purity, or target properties, including uncertainty.
3. **Experiment selector:** choose the next experiment using predicted improvement, information gained, and laboratory cost.

A practical first implementation could use:

- An LLM for literature extraction, retrieval assistance, and structured protocol preparation.
- A Gaussian process or tree ensemble as an initial outcome model.
- Bayesian optimization for experiment selection within the lab's executable constraints.

More elaborate generative architectures should be justified by improved measured performance. Multiple recipes can produce the same target, so the system should support alternative candidate routes.

Thermodynamic information may help rank recipes but cannot alone establish reaction pathways or outcomes. Kinetics-aware modeling is a possible source of scientific contribution. The discussion cited a 2026 study combining transport predictions with reaction modeling: [Nature Materials paper](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41563-026-02596-5).

## Experimental validation and fair comparison

Agree on the success threshold with the collaborator before evaluating the method. For phase purity, specify the characterization procedure and treatment of measurement uncertainty and amorphous content where relevant.

Proposed comparisons:

- Literature retrieval or an expert-selected starting recipe.
- Conventional Bayesian optimization using the same allowed variables.
- The proposed approach using literature knowledge and impurity feedback.

Methods should receive comparable experimental budgets and initial information. Count and retain every attempt, including partial successes and operational failures. Repeat promising recipes on independent days or batches.

The strongest endpoint is **experiments and elapsed laboratory time required to reach a predefined, verified outcome**. Also assess reproducibility and final material quality. Agreement with a published recipe is an auxiliary metric, since multiple valid recipes can exist.

For retrospective evaluation, keep records from the same paper or closely related campaign together. Evaluate unfamiliar targets or chemistry groups separately and audit near duplicates. Distinguish:

1. Recovering known recipes.
2. Predicting outcomes for unfamiliar recipes.
3. Improving real experiments by following model recommendations.

Only the third directly demonstrates practical synthesis-planning value. Correlations in literature data do not establish that changing a processing condition causes an improvement.

## Illustrative project schedule

The following **6–12-month schedule** assumes regular lab access and is not a user commitment:

- **Months 1–2:** choose chemistry and measurements, audit relevant data, and quantify experimental variability.
- **Months 3–4:** reproduce retrieval and optimization baselines and build the initial outcome model.
- **Months 5–9:** run prospective comparisons, collecting all results and updating the model.
- **Months 10–12:** assess reproducibility and transfer to additional targets; release data and code where possible.

## Next decisions to make with the collaborator

1. Material family and target compositions/phases.
2. Solid-state versus solution synthesis, including the controllable variables.
3. One measurable primary success criterion and available characterization.
4. Weekly experiment capacity, turnaround time, and total budget, including controls and repeats.
5. Availability and quality of historical lab records, especially unsuccessful attempts.

The immediate recommendation was to meet with the collaborator and choose **one material family, one measurable success criterion, and a realistic weekly experimental budget**. These choices determine the model and the research claim that can be convincingly tested.

## 2026-09-24 — User asks how to accelerate website contributions

The main throughput bottleneck is source-backed extraction, variant/sample assignment and independent review, followed by integrating already-reviewed candidates into the shared MatterSyn schema. Page rendering is generated from structured records and reusable Reader components; hosting is batched. A paper's reported precursor masses, conditions, product characterization, SI/figure assignment, and conflicting claims need traceable, field-level validation. A rendered material page does not by itself mean that every synthesis–structure pair or model-training label is verified.

Use language models/OCR to draft table and method extractions, suggest candidate sample/figure links, normalize likely fields, and flag missing/conflicting values. Keep source locators for every field and independently verify the output against the original paper/SI before integration. Parallelize on disjoint paper scopes with separately frozen audits; reserve one integration lane to map passed evidence into the shared material/record schema and preserve conflicting source statements. Extend reusable chemistry/molecule, apparatus and material-page components instead of hand-building a new frontend for each paper. GPU/API capacity can speed draft extraction and OCR; it does not establish uncertain sample linkage or remove the evidence check.

Historical measurement: nine audited/published source scopes in a selected, concurrent cohort took 12.8685 elapsed hours, about 16.8 per day by arithmetic. The cohort is small and overlapping; this is not a whole-corpus forecast. The user's 500-per-day target is roughly 30 times that observed arithmetic rate and remains unmet. A local-model draft lane once handled roughly 108–137 short sources per hour, while a separate verification run missed a known 300 K versus 230–300 °C conflict. Do not cite raw model throughput as audited throughput or promise 500 per day from adding GPUs alone. Benchmark complete paper-to-reader cycles and capacity by evidence-rich source group before forecasting.

The immediate preserved integration candidate is Williamson et al., ACS Nano (2021), DOI 10.1021/acsnano.1c00502, CoNi2S4. The isolated R5 display candidate and its independent targeted reader delta audit are bound by SHA256 6aa640e4c77b2449ad96e2a6e1cac55714ce7fdd912a2aeddf9e7742b5b07ab1 and audit SHA256 4a547f4bdafa15e3e93cc10e192b87c5e10e753ee96301353a7056b471d8a4d0; 168/168 scoped checks passed. This closes the R5 caption/molecular-drawing delta only. It does not itself promote the candidate into the canonical record schema, create additional distinct recipes, establish exact sample-coordinate pairs, or approve model-training examples. Candidate extraction retains source conflicts and unresolved condition/sample links; apply the shared-record mapping and its normal release review before publication. This follow-up bottleneck note is not itself a new paper or release.
